みずほ経済 EXPRESS

日本の原油輸入価格は今後さらに上昇へ 
～物価上振れリスクが強まれば、一段の利上げ加速も～

調査部 主任エコノミスト 東深澤 武史 
池田 亮平 
エコノミスト 阿部 大樹 
シニア日本経済エコノミスト 越山 祐資 
080-1069-4919  takeshi.higashifukasawa@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 日銀は物価上振れ抑制に舵。中東情勢に起因する物価押し上げリスクは予断を許さず 
9 月日銀会合後の会見で植田総裁は「政策局面が変化した」と述べた（図表 1） 。これまで日銀は、
基調的物価の押し上げを金融政策運営の重点としてきたが、足元では 2％前後の物価上昇が定着しつ
つあるとみられる。こうしたもとで、金融政策運営の重点は物価上振れの回避に移っており、そのた
めに緩和的な金融環境をなるべく早く脱することが重視される。日銀の基本的なスタンスが、連続利
上げや、0.25％の利上げ幅を上回る大幅利上げは回避するのが望ましいというものであることも踏ま
えると、今後の利上げペースは 3 カ月に一回程度、0.25％ずつが基本になるだろう 1。 
ただし、金融政策の重点が物価上振れ回避にシフトしているなかでは、よりフォワードルッキング
な政策運営が求められる 2。したがって、 原油調達コストの上昇や円安など、 物価上振れリスクの強ま
り次第で利上げペースが速まる可能性がある。先行きの物価を押し上げる主なリスク要因として、日
図表1 9月会合後の総裁会見のポイント 図表2 WTI原油先物

### Page 2

2

銀は、原油高等の中東情勢影響、AI 関連需要の強さ、円安の三点を挙げている。このうち、原油価格
は足元で再び上昇しており、先行きの不確実性も大きい。中東情勢は、物価を取り巻く現下最大のリ
スクだろう。 
■ 不安定な情勢下で再び原油価格が上昇。タンカー運賃も急騰しダブルパンチの様相 
WTI原油先物は、9月に入って一時1バレル=100ドル台と、5月中旬以来の水準に上昇した （図表2） 。
足元では原油価格は幾分低下しているものの、水準としては依然高い状態が続いている。背景には、
中東産原油の供給懸念が強まったことがある。7月下旬以降、 サウジアラビアとイエメン・フーシ派に
よる戦闘が開始し、 ホルムズ海峡を代替する重要な原油輸送ルートとして活用されてきたバブ ・ エル ・
マンデブ海峡の通航リスクが高まった。 また、9月に入り、 サウジアラビアの原油パイプラインが攻撃
を受けて停止したと伝わると、中東産原油の域外輸送が一段と困難になるとの見方が広がった。 
中東情勢が再度悪化するなかで上昇しているのは原油価格だけではない。原油価格の上昇を大幅に
上回る勢いで、原油タンカーの運賃が急騰している（図表3）。8月以降に上昇基調が鮮明となり、9月
中旬には一時、中東情勢悪化前（2026年初）の約46倍、情勢悪化後の3～7月頃と比べても約3.2倍に達
した。 海峡航行リスクが高まるもと、 積み替えや迂回によって原油輸送の効率が悪化しているためだ。
海事データを活用して商品市場を分析しているベルギーの調査会社Kplerによると、 ホルムズ海峡の内
側にあるペルシャ湾から原油を運び出す船と、海峡の外側から最終目的地まで運ぶ船を分け、洋上で
原油を積み替える方式が広がっているようだ。 長距離輸送を担う船が危険な航路を通らずに済む反面、
積み替え作業や待機に時間を要するため、船を次の輸送に回しにくくなる。また、危険な海域を避け
るために遠回りする輸送も増え、船が一度の航海に費やす日数も長くなっている。より多くの輸送資
源が必要となったことで船舶需給がひっ迫し、タンカー運賃が大きく押し上げられている格好だ。

図表3 原油タンカーの運賃 図表4 原油輸入単価と原油価格

■ 今後は日本の原油調達コストが上昇。企業間取引価格は反転加速を予想 
原油価格の上昇に原油タンカー運賃の急騰が重なり、先行きの日本の原油輸入単価（単位量あたり
の原油調達コスト）は一段と上昇する見込みだ3。 中東産原油が日本に輸送されるまでには1～2カ月程
度の期間を要するため、8月中旬以降の原油価格上昇 ・原油タンカー運賃上昇は10月以降の原油輸入単
価を押し上げ、その後国内の企業間取引価格へと影響が波及していくと考えられる。足元の原油価格
とタンカー運賃を踏まえると、10月の原油輸入単価は1バレル＝約140ドル程度と、中東情勢悪化後で
最も高い水準に上昇する可能性が高い（図表4）。この影響を輸入全体の平均価格（円建てベース）で
みると、10月の伸びは前年比＋29％程度と、中東情勢悪化後に最も輸入物価の伸びが加速した6・7月
に迫る勢いになる計算だ。さらに、その後は、石油化学製品などの関連財を中心に、国内の企業間取
引価格が上昇しよう。 足元で上昇圧力が一服しつつある国内企業物価は再び加速し、11月頃に同＋7％
台の伸びになると予想する（図表5）。 
加えて、原油輸入金額が膨らんで貿易収支が悪化すれば、円安圧力が強まる可能性がある。原油の
代替調達が進展した6月以降は1兆円に迫る貿易赤字が続いているが、特に10月以降は原油輸入単価が
上昇するもとで本邦貿易赤字が拡大する公算が大きく、実需面からの円安圧力が強まりやすい（図表
6） 。 足元の為替相場は、 米国の利上げ観測を背景に円安に振れやすい地合いだ。 そこに実需の動きが
重なり、1ドル＝160円を上回るようなドル高円安が進行すれば、輸入物価の上振れを通じて、国内企
業物価の伸びが一層高まるリスクがある。 
■ 物価上昇圧力が長期化する場合は利上げペース加速も視野に 
以上のように、当面の企業間取引価格に対しては押し上げ圧力が持続する公算が大きい。物価上昇
圧力が長期化するほど消費者物価への価格転嫁姿勢も強まる可能性があるだけに、日銀は物価上振れ
に対する高い警戒レベルを維持することになろう。 
仮に物価上振れリスクが大きく高まる場合には、 連続利上げ ・大幅利上げの可能性も排除できない。
中東情勢の混乱が長期化し、27年以降も原油輸入単価が一段と上振れることが見込まれる場合、ある

### Page 4

4

いは1ドル=160円を持続的に上回る水準まで円安が進展する場合には 、連続利上げや大幅利上げのシ
ナリオが現実味を帯びてくるだろう。

1 中立金利の水準は不透明であり、利上げの影響はタイムラグをともなって現れる。連続利上げ・大幅利上げによって意図せず
金融引き締めに転じれば、経済へのマイナス影響が必要以上に強まるリスクがある。 
2 中東情勢悪化以降で最初の利上げを決定した 6 月会合を振り返っても、日銀は川上の企業物価の加速を理由の一つとして利上
げに踏み切っている。6 月会合の声明文において日銀は、国内企業物価上昇率が前年比＋6.3％（5 月時点の速報ベース）に加速
したことを踏まえ、 「企業間取引におけるやや速いスピードでの価格転嫁が、消費者段階における幅広い品目の価格上昇に波及
していく可能性がある」と評価した。 
3 中東情勢悪化以降、日本の原油輸入単価は国際市況から大きく上振れている。背景として、主な代替調達先である米国産原油
が需給ひっ迫の懸念等から値上がりし、割高になったことなどが挙げられる。経済産業省発表によると、秋口以降は中東産原油
の調達が増加し、割高な米国産原油に対する依存度は低下すると見込まれるものの、本文記載のように中東産原油の輸送コスト
が大幅に高まっていることから、先行きも割高感の解消は見込みづらい。

### Page 3

Mediatek (2454.TW)
30 October 2026 Citi Research
3
Structural OPM upward trend — Management expects 4Q26 to mark the 
beginning of a structural improvement in OPM, supported by rapidly scaling data 
center ASIC revenue and operating leverage. Management indicated that the OPM 
could exceed 20% in 2027. Meanwhile, selective pricing adjustments across 
traditional businesses should help offset rising supply chain costs. Beyond data 
centers, Smart Edge should benefit from new AI PCs, AI tablets, automotive 
platforms and networking applications, including continued collaboration with 
NVIDIA on next-generation AI computing products. While smartphone demand 
remains relatively subdued, management expects the expanding AI business to 
increasingly dominate MediaTek’s overall growth and earnings trajectory.
Second-Generation ASIC on Track for 1Q28 
Production, with a Second Customer 
Management expressed increasing confidence in achieving the upper end of its 15–
20% market share target for the estimated US$80bn data center AI accelerator 
ASIC market in 2027, implying approximately US$16bn in potential revenue. This 
represents a substantial increase from the revised US$2.5bn target for 2026, 
supported by strong demand visibility and continued supply chain expansion. 
Management emphasized that securing adequate foundry, packaging and other 
critical component capacity remains essential to meeting customer requirements. 
Importantly, the revenue opportunity reflects MediaT ek’s increasing participation in 
the value chain, encompassing advanced process technologies, packaging, high-
speed interconnects and custom silicon integration. Despite recent market 
concerns over AI infrastructure financing and CSP capital expenditure 
sustainability, management remains confident that AI compute demand will 
continue growing over the next several years, particularly for customized 
accelerators offering superior performance per watt and total cost of ownership.
Figure 1. Mediatek - 3Q26 results overview 
© 2026 Citigroup Inc. No redistribution without Citigroup’s written permission.
Source: Citi Research, Bloomberg
Management reiterated that its second AI accelerator project remains on schedule 
for high-volume production in 1Q28, despite market concerns regarding design 
complexity, advanced packaging yields and potential delays. The new accelerator is 
expected to deliver multiple times the performance per watt of the first-generation 
product, significantly improving customer TCO and strengthening Media T ek’s 
competitive positioning . Management acknowledged manufacturing challenges 
associated with its U.S. IDM packaging partner but highlighted progress in 
identifying key yield constraints and implementing corrective measures, expressing 
high confidence in further yield improvements. Importantly, Media T ek also 
confirmed a separate design win with a second, non-Chinese data center

Mediatek (2454.TW)
30 October 2026 Citi Research
4
customer, with initial revenue expected in 2028. The company is already
collaborating with its existing customer on subsequent-generation products,
although specifications are still being finalized. Management expects another year
of strong revenue growth in 2028 but declined to provide specific revenue guidance
or customer contribution estimates.
Expanding competitive advantages through advanced node

design/packaging, SerDes and NVIDIA NVLink Fusion
MediaT ek continues investing in advanced technology capabilities, including 448G
SerDes, advanced packaging, HBM integration and co-packaged optics (CPO), to
establish a competitive advantage in increasingly complex AI infrastructure
designs. Management emphasized that its differentiation comes not from
individual technologies but from integrating them into high-performance, power-
efficient and commercially viable solutions. More importantly, its partnership with

NVIDIA through NVLink Fusion enables customers to integrate custom XPUs into
NVIDIA’s rack-scale ecosystem, while its support for UALink and Ethernet-based
architectures provides greater deployment flexibility. MediaT ek is expanding
beyond individual silicon components toward subsystems and rack-level
solutions, shortening customers’ development cycles and accelerating time to
revenue. Management indicated encouraging engagement with multiple potential

customers and expects broader adoption opportunities. The 448G SerDes
development is progressing well and is expected to support both existing
accelerator programs and future customers, with management not anticipating it
to become a critical schedule bottleneck.
Structural OPM expansion trend; Reiterate Buy
Management expects 4Q26 to mark the beginning of a structural improvement in
operating profitability, supported by rapidly scaling data center ASIC revenue and

operating leverage. Although ASIC gross margins are expected to remain below
MediaT ek’s traditional corporate average, the significantly larger revenue
contribution and more favorable operating expense leverage should drive
meaningful operating margin expansion. Management indicated that the operating
margin could exceed 20% in 2027, potentially somewhat higher, compared with its
historical mid-teen level. Meanwhile, selective pricing adjustments across

traditional businesses should help offset rising supply chain costs, although these
increases are primarily intended to preserve profitability rather than expand gross
margins. Beyond data centers, Smart Edge should benefit from new AI PCs, AI
tablets, automotive platforms and networking applications, including continued
collaboration with NVIDIA on next-generation AI computing products. While
smartphone demand remains relatively subdued, management expects the

expanding AI business to increasingly dominate MediaT ek’s overall growth and
earnings trajectory.
Given MediaT ek’s better operating leverage, we lift our earnings projection for
2027 /2028 5%/5%, mainly reflecting better OPM. We maintain our Buy rating with
an unchanged TP of NT$6,800, implying 29x our 2027 /28 EPS estimates.

### Page 9

Mediatek (2454.TW)
30 October 2026 Citi Research
9
Mediatek
Company description
Mediatek (MTK) is a fabless IC company that designs SoC for smartphone,
smart TV, and digital media devices. It also develops chips for connectivity,
networking, smart home gadgets, and automotive sectors. It was founded in
1997, is listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange, and is headquartered in Taiwan.
It has sales and research subsidiaries in mainland China, Singapore, India,

the US, Japan, Korea, Denmark, and England. It primarily sells its products in
China and other emerging-market countries.
Investment strategy
We rate Mediatek shares as Buy. We expect Mediatek to ride on 5G migration
in emerging markets and market share gain of its high-end Dimensity series in
China market as well. In particular, we see strong growth potential from its AI
ASIC, NTN-satellite business in the long term. We believe solid fundamentals

and strong operating leverage will support Mediatek’s margins outlook.
Valuation
Our NT$6,800 target price (rounded) for Mediatek is based on 29x P/E to
2027 /2028 EPS, which is 1-std above its five-year average forward P/E given
the promising outlook from its AI ASIC business starting from 2H26. We
believe our target PE is also justified by Mediatek's global and regional peers'
trading at an average of c32x PER. AI ASIC development with CSPs should

provide more upside from 2027 onwards. We also see Mediatek as well
prepared for 6G and 6G+NTN Satellite communication. Despite lukewarm
smartphone overall deamand, we expect Mediatek to benefit from 5G
migration in emerging markets and its Dimensity series should also gain
shares in flagship models. Non-smartphone business is also seeing
stabilizing TV and Io T demand with Mediatek outgrowing the overall market
on its better technology in WiFi 7 and connectivity.

Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) weaker-than-expected growth from AI ASIC business; 2)
more severe competition in the smartphone segment; 3) slower overall
demand related to macroeconomic risks, especially for smartphone demand;
and 4) further price hikes from foundries.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment
rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered

stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating
and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also

suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price
chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in

exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the
expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility

or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:
Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside

call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is
moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day

period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be
different from and does not affect a stock’s fundamental equity rating, which reflects a longer-term total absolute return

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We update our view on Advantech after 3Q26 results 
(released on 4 November).

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results beat. Advantech’s 3Q26 earnings of 
TWD6.2bn (EPS: TWD7.15) beat our estimate and BBG consensus by 
26% and 28%, respectively, driven by record-high OPM (20.9%) and better-
than-expected non-ops (gains from its Japan plant’s asset disposal). 
However, GM fell 1pp QoQ to 36.7%, mainly due to raw-material cost 
inflation. Management said CPU, memory and other component costs rose 
to 70% (vs. 30% previously). Although the company could pass on higher 
costs, it couldn’t maintain its blended GM on an incremental sales.

Business update. Looking ahead, Advantech remains positive on its 4Q26 
and 2027 outlook, supported by a c.USD2.5bn order backlog and a 3Q26 
B/B ratio of 1.64 (vs. 1.6 in October). Management said cost-inflation-driven 
demand pull-ins were limited in 9M26, but order visibility has extended as 
clients seek greater allocations. Advantech sees AI infrastructure, 
semiconductors, factory automation and medical equipment as key growth 
drivers, with shipments in these applications expected to grow c.15% YoY 
in 2026E and into 2027E, based on our forecasts. Edge AI remains its 
fastest-growing application: its revenue contribution rose to 24.5% in 9M26, 
with c.109% YoY growth, vs. 22.5% in 1H26 and the company’s c.30% 
target for 2026. Growth has been driven mainly by higher ASPs, but we 
expect unit growth to become the primary driver in 2027E as ASPs lap the 
increase in 2026 already. In the near term, while management guides its 
revenue would decrease by single-digit % QoQ due to fewer working days 
over Christmas holiday period in 4Q26, but we believe this is too 
conservative. We expect 4Q26E revenue to be broadly flat QoQ, as a solid 
backlog is on hand. We also expect gross margin to remain similar to 3Q26 
amid ongoing cost inflation. Management reported ASP increases of 10-
20% for CPUs and 15-20% for PCBs, while SSD and memory prices have 
been relatively stable recently. We expect shortages to ease gradually, 
particularly for memory, but to persist for CPUs and CCLs.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 7-22% to reflect 
Advantech’s 3Q26 results. We reaffirm our Outperform (2) call and raise 
our 12M TP to TWD750 (from TWD700), based on a lower PER of 26x 
(prior: 30x; slightly higher than the mid-point of its past-3-year range of 20-
37x), as we are closer to the end of the up-cycle, and applied to our 1-year 
forward EPS. Key downside risk: slower-than-expected demand recovery.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 10-17% above the consensus, 
likely due to our more bullish outlook on Advantech’s revenue.

4 November 2026

3Q26 results: positive tone towards 2027

➢ Up-cycle will last into 2027, with limited early pull-in shipments in 2026 
➢ Key growth drivers in 2026 include ASP hike and fast Edge AI growth 
➢ Reaffirming our Outperform (2) rating; raising 12-month TP to TWD750

Company profile 
Founded in 1981 and listed in 1999, Advantech is the largest industrial PC (IPC) maker worldwide in 
terms of embedded board/system sales, followed by the company’s main competitors, Germany-based 
Kontron and US-based Radisys. The company mainly provides embedded computing boards, chassis, 
industrial computers, applied computers and design-in services. The businesses are categorised into 4 
segments: Embedded design-in, Industrial IoT, Service IoT, and Global services.

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
What's new? Earlier today, a fire broke out at Panasonic's (covered by Kazuo
Yoshikawa) CCL factory in Hsinchu, Taiwan. The fire was extinguished before noon
(see Reuters' report), but Panasonic has yet to issue an official statement in
response to the incident.
Our view: Our checks indicate that this plant has monthly capacity of ~200K sheets

and primarily produces M6/7 and some lower-end BT substrate CCLs. Panasonic also
operates CCL sites in Fukushima, Japan; Guangzhou and Suzhou, China; and
Ayutthaya, Thailand, which remains under construction. Most high-end M7/8 CCL is
produced in Fukushima, while the China sites supply much of its mainstream CCL.
Our supply chain checks suggest Panasonic is reallocating capacity toward high-end
CCL for AI applications – particularly a key US hyperscaler’s in-house ASICs, as high-

end CCL supply remains tight. Although the Hsinchu plant is immaterial to group
capacity, the fire could accelerate this shift, potentially driving near-term customer
order overflow to Taiwanese CCL competitors.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
A fire at Panasonic's CCL factory in Hsinchu may push near-term orderflow to
Taiwanese competitors.
Within our coverage, we see EMC as most likely to receive orders, followed by
TUC and Shengyi Tech (EW).
EMC is also a high-end CCL supplier to Panasonic’s key ASIC customer.
Valuations are attractive for EMC and TUC, and we remain OW on both.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Document 171: 261105_daiwa_SDI

Source PDF: all report pdf/261105_daiwa_SDI.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: SDI reported its 3Q26 results after market hours on 5 November. Its 3Q26 EPS 
was TWD1.94, beating our EPS estimate (TWD1.79) but missing the Bloomberg 
consensus (TWD2.07).

We have a Buy (1) rating on SDI with a 12-month TP of TWD265, based on a PER of 35x 
on our 1-year-forward EPS. For more information on the company, please refer to our 
latest flash, 2Q26 review: net profit beat expectations, on 7 August 2026.

What’s the impact 
• 3Q26 results beat our estimate but missed the Bloomberg consensus. SDI’s 3Q26 
revenue of TWD4,020m (+12.9% QoQ, +58.1% YoY) beat our and the consensus 
estimates by 12.4% and 2.4%, mainly due to the better-than-expected auto segment 
and rush orders for the consumer segment. Its gross margin came in at 20.5% in 3Q26 
(vs. 20.7% in 2Q26 and 12.4% in 3Q25), missing both our (20.7%) and the consensus 
(21.5%) estimates, which we attribute to a lower-than-expected yield rate and OT 
expense despite positive factors such as price hikes, a higher copper price and FX. Its 
operating profit of TWD517m (+24.0% QoQ, +399.0% YoY) beat our estimate by 13.4%, 
but was in line with the consensus estimate. Its net profit came in at TWD383m, which 
also beat our estimate (TWD339m) but missed the Bloomberg consensus (TWD400m). 
Its 3Q26 EPS was TWD1.94. For 3Q26 revenue, electronics accounted for 91% 
(+14.2% QoQ, +67.3% YoY) and stationery accounted for 9% (+1.6% QoQ, +1.6% YoY). 
Within electronics, the product mix was: auto 47% (+14.2% QoQ, +63.8% YoY), 
industrial 15% (+14.2% QoQ, +19.5% YoY), consumer 28% (+6.6% QoQ, +67.3% YoY), 
AI lead frame 7% (+33.2% QoQ) and others 3% (+71.3% QoQ, +67.3% YoY).

SDI: 3Q26 results vs. Daiwa and the Bloomberg forecasts 
(TWDm) SDI’s 3Q26 results   Daiwa forecast  Differ  Bloomberg consensus  Differ

Gross profit                          824                         742  11.2%                                    844  -2.3%

Source: Company, Bloomberg, Daiwa forecasts

• Bright lead frame outlook with rising AI lead frame. SDI expects 4Q26 revenue to be 
flat to slightly up QoQ and 20%+ revenue YoY growth for its 2027 lead frame business, 
due to an intact order outlook from auto, industrial and AI. It also expects its AI lead 
frame revenue is to reach 10%+ of 2027 revenue.

What we recommend 
We have a Buy (1) rating on SDI with a 12-month TP of TWD265, based on a PER of 35x 
on our 1-year-forward EPS. The stock is currently trading at PERs of 25.2x/19.1x, based 
on our 2026/27E EPS, vs. its past-3-year one-year forward PER trading range of 12-72x

3Q26 OP 2.7조, 당사 추정치 35% 상회 
서프라이즈의 근거가 판매보증비 감소에서 발생. 3Q26 기말환율의 낙폭 (-12% qoq)으로 
인한 역설적 비용절감의 규모가 전년동기 대비 1.2조 원을 넘어서며 환율의 조사모삼 효과
로 만들어진 컨센서스 상회 실적. 
긍정적인 부분: 컨틴전시, 신차 효과, 인센티브, 생산 정상화 
지속적인 컨틴전시 체제를 유지하며 비용절감 실천 (=지속된 요소). 4분기 추가적으로 나아
질 요인은 1) 내수 신차효과로 판가 상승 및 유기적 수익성 개선, 2) 공장 가동률 정상화를 
통한 고정비 부담 절감, 3) 해외시장 신차효과를 통한 딜러 인센티브 절감. 
경계할 부분: 재료비, 환율, BEV 판매비중 확대 
재료비 측면에서 1) 2Q26 시작된 미국-이란 전쟁 관련 원재료비 상승은 4Q26F에도 하락
세를 보이지 못할 것이며, 2) 구리, 철강 등의 금속 원자재의 가격 상승 또한 4Q26F 지속될 
것. 또한 3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (mtd 1,338원) 효과로 
환 관련 감익 본격화, IONIQ 3의 출시는 인센티브 절감인 동시에 믹스 관점에서 부정적. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 440,000원으로 소폭 상향 
목표주가 상향 근거는 컨틴전시 체제 유지를 통한 FY27F 자동차사업 부문 이익 예상치 상
향. 부정적 외생변수 (환율, 재료비)가 지속된다는 전제 하에 FY27F OPM 하락을 예상하나, 
(FY26F 5.6%, FY27F 5.2%) 향후 신차효과와 생산 정상화에 따른 자동차 사업부문 수익성 
반등의 여력 존재. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) CES 전후 BD 관련 기대감, 2) 신차 출시 이후 견
조한 판매량, 3) 환율, 원재료비 등 외생변수의 개선.

Document 222: [005380] 현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005380] 현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 3분기, 물량 감소에도 확인된 이익 방어력 
현대차의 3Q26 매출액은 45조 1,100억원(Y oY -3.4%), 영업이익은 2조 6,770억원
(Y oY +5.5%), OPM 5.9%(Y oY +0.5%p)를 기록했다. 국내 파업 및 영업일수 감소 영
향으로 글로벌 도매 판매량은 92.1만대(Y oY -11.3%)까지 감소했으나, HEV 중심의 
고부가 차종 Mix 개선, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비(Y oY -1.2조원) 감소 효과
로 수익성은 오히려 개선됐다. 특히 HEV 판매 비중은 18.2%(Y oY +2.7%p)까지 상
승했다. 반면 EV 판매는 전년 대비 감소세가 이어지며 지역별 수요 둔화와 경쟁 심
화 영향이 지속됐다. 
연간 눈높이는 낮아졌지만, 믹스 개선은 지속 
현대차는 글로벌 수요 둔화와 생산 차질을 반영해 2026년 글로벌 도매 판매 목표를 
기존 415.8만대에서 400만대로, 영업이익률 가이던스를 기존 6.3~7.3%에서 
5.5~6.5%로 하향 조정했다. 다만 북미를 중심으로 HEV 판매가 견조하게 유지되고 
있으며, 하반기 주요 신차 투입과 전동화 라인업 확대를 통해 믹스 개선 기조는 이
어질 전망이다. 중장기적으로는 로보택시 공급 확대와 Atlas 상용화 등 Physical AI 
사업도 계획대로 진행 중으로, 단기 판매 성장 둔화에도 자동차 본업의 수익성 방어
와 신사업 모멘텀은 유효한 것으로 판단한다. 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 460,000원 하향 
글로벌 완성차 업체들의 수익성 부담은 확대되고 있다. 3분기 자동차 부문 영업이익
률은 Mercedes-Benz 2.5%, Stellantis 0.7%, Volvo -5.6%, VW -7.8%를 기록했다. 
유럽 내 BEV 판매 확대에도 중국 전기차 업체들의 공격적인 해외 진출과 가격 경
쟁, 중국 시장 부진이 동시에 나타나면서 전통 OEM의 수익성 압박이 심화되고 있
다. 이러한 환경에서 현대차는 HEV 중심의 유연한 파워트레인 전략과 북미 중심의 
Mix 개선을 통해 5% 후반의 수익성을 유지하고 있다는 점이 상대적으로 부각된다.

목표주가 (하향) 460,000원

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일

3Q26Pre: 환율 하락에도 성장 흐름 유지 
매출액 1조 3,477억원(+7.2%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 5,957억원(-6.0%, OPM 
44.2%)로 컨센서스에 부합할 것으로 전망. 3분기 원달러 평균 환율은 1,423원으로 전분기 
평균 환율 대비 약 5.2% 하락에도 불구하고 성장 흐름 지속. 
2분기 노조 파업으로 인한 영향은 3분기 생산을 진행했으며 4분기 매출에 반영될 예정으로 
연간 실적에 미치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상. GSK로부터 인수한 록빌 공장도 안정적
으로 생산이 이루어지고 있으며 CAPA 여유분은 추가 수주를 진행 중으로 28~29년 이후 
완전 가동 전망. 
4Q, 6공장 건설과 폴리펩타이드 인수 
유상 증자 관련 투자 설명서 상 투자 계획에 따르면 6공장 투자는 26년부터 29년까지 진행 
될 예정으로 29년 가동 목표. 6공장은 5공장과 동일한 18만 리터 규모로 건설되며, 연내 투
자 집행을 시작함에 따라 건설 발표도 임박한 것으로 추정. 동사는 일정 수준 이상 기존 공
장 수주를 확보한 후 후속 공장 건설을 진행해오고 있어 non-binding 수주 계약의 본계약 
전환도 순차적으로 이루어질 것으로 전망. 
폴리펩타이드 인수도 현재 공개 매수를 진행 중에 있으며 연내 마무리될 것으로 예상. 
1H26 폴리펩타이드 매출은 2.37억 유로(+41.6%)로 GLP-1 agonist 등 펩타이드 의약품 
수요 증가에 따라 높은 성장 지속. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원으로 하향 
매수 의견을 유지하며 목표 주가는 기존 210만원에서 200만원으로 하향. 목표 주가 하향은 
유상 증자에 따른 EPS 희석에 기인. 글로벌 항체 CDMO 수요가 여전히 견조하며, 폴리펩
타이드 인수로 펩타이드로의 확장에 따른 추가적인 성장 기대. 주주 대상 유상 증자 영향과 
파업 우려 등이 있으나 노사 합의 시 주가 반등 전망.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN DWELLING
APPROVALS BULLETIN
Units lift the headline
2 November 2026
Key points
• Total dwelling approvals rose 5.1%mth
in September, although the annual pace
moved into contraction at –0.8%yr with
the detail suggesting the underlying
cycle is starting to turn down.

• The headline increase was driven by
another volatile rise in private unit
approvals (+16.6%) which more than
offset a significant decline in private
detached house approvals (–5.3%).
• Units drove gains in NSW and
Queensland. WA, SA and Victoria all
recorded declines. Detached dwelling
approvals fell across all states.
• The total value of residential approvals
fell 2.8%mth but a sharp rise in non-
residential approvals – led by the
data centres – saw the total value of

building approvals rise 13%mth.
• Interest rate increases, weakness in
established housing markets, rising costs
and pessimistic consumer sentiment
are likely to remain headwinds.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Total dwelling approvals rose 5.1%mth in September, while
the annual pace moved into contraction at –0.8%yr. The
result was well above both Westpac’s expectation for a
7.0%mth decline and the market expectation for a 2.0%mth
rise. Unit approvals continued to drive headline volatility,

surging in September, while detached dwellings, which
provide a better read on the underlying trend, recorded a
sizeable decline.
Note that most figures in the charts and table are presented on
a rolling-three month basis to smooth out monthly volatility.
Private detached dwelling approvals fell 5.3%mth in
September, unwinding August’s upwardly-revised 5.4%mth
rise (+1.7ppts). Recent resilience has kept the segment
elevated, with approvals remaining above 10,000 and the

annual pace at 9.6%yr. However, the quarterly profile provides
a clearer read on the underlying trend and suggests detached
dwelling approvals are beginning to respond to recent interest
rate increases (see here). Approvals rose just 0.9%qtr in
September vs 3.1%qtr in June and 5.9%qtr in March.
Private unit approvals remained volatile, rising 16.6%mth in
September following 21.7%mth decline in August. Strength
was led by high-rise approvals, while low-mid-rise approvals

recorded a more modest increase. Despite the monthly rise,
the annual pace fell further into contraction at –20.5%yr.
Among the states, NSW recorded the strongest result,
with private approvals rising 35.3%mth led by a surge in
private unit approvals. Queensland also recorded an increase
(+9.3%mth), while WA (–2.9%mth), SA (–3.5%mth) and
Victoria (–29.0%mth) all declined.
In value terms, residential building approvals fell 2.8%mth,
driven by a 3.3%mth decline in new residential approvals,

partially offset by a 0.8%mth rise in renovation activity.
Non-residential approvals once again reversed sharply, rising
45.9%mth, as the data centre-dominated ‘other commercial’
segment recorded an 8-fold increase on a non-seasonally
adjusted basis. The total value of building approvals rose
13.0%mth in September. On a non-seasonally adjusted basis,
the value of building approvals rose 8.3%mth overall, but
declined 3.0%mth when excluding the ‘other commercial’

segment.
September’s result reinforces the importance of looking
through headline volatility in unit approvals. While total
approvals rose in the month, detached dwelling approvals
slowed on a quarterly basis and fell across all states, pointing
to softer underlying momentum. The annual pace of total
approvals also moved into contraction for the first time
since January. Interest rate increases, the sharp downturn
in established housing markets and rising costs are likely to

weigh on further growth, with pessimistic consumer sentiment
an additional headwind (see here).
Detached approvals point to softer momentum

Document 346: FX Daily_ A dollar downturn lacks the rate ingredient

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Daily_ A dollar downturn lacks the rate ingredient.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

The post-midterm dollar correction requires a worsening of USD
rate diﬀerentials to turn into a downturn. But as oil prices fall,
other central banks are facing similar or even larger dovish
repricing than the Fed. Data is key now, but indications are that
they won’t turn USD-negative soon. Today, expect a hike in the
UK and rate holds in Norway and Mexico

Francesco Pesole, Frantisek Taborsky and Chris Turner
We don't see much room for the dollar drop to extend
USD: Front-end rates should prevent a
bigger decline
The Democratic sweep at the midterms delivered the negative
dollar impact we had expected. But as we argued before the vote,
we didn’t see a case for a bigger USD downturn. In our view, rates
need to start moving against the dollar to justify a greater
adjustment, unless idiosyncratic US asset underperformance fuels
the move. But fears that a Democratic sweep would hit tech stocks
didn’t materialise. Instead, a potential relief that the midterm risk

### Page 2

event is out of the way, alongside plummeting oil prices, is fuelling
an equity rally.
The key hurdle for a bigger USD downturn is that lower oil prices are
not unequivocally USD-negative. Let’s look at yesterday’s moves in
rates. As crude dropped 8%, two-year USD swap rates fell 5bp,
broadly in line with CAD, AUD and SEK swaps. EUR, GBP and NZD
2yr swap rates, however, fell more, by around 8-10bp. To us, that is
the most compelling argument against chasing the dollar much

lower. Poorer US data and/or a more dovish Fed remain the
necessary conditions for front-end USD rates to fall more than
peers, and by extension for a broader USD downturn.
Instead, data has remained broadly supportive of Fed tightening
expectations. Yesterday, the ISM services index was a tad softer
than expected but remains well into expansionary territory. The
employment subindex rose to 51.4, the highest since February.
Paired with strong ADP payrolls at 86k, that’s likely to reinforce

expectations for a robust payroll print tomorrow – consensus is 65k,
but the whisper number has risen to 70k.
Today’s data highlights are the Challenger jobs cut ﬁgures for
October and 3Q unit labour costs. We favour a stabilisation around
101.0-101.50 in DXY in the coming days, rather than another break
lower.
Francesco Pesole
Elsewhere, Banxico announces its rate decision later today. The
peso has been the worst-performing EM currency over the last two

months after Banxico said it would not be following the Fed with a
rate hike in September. In fact, some are looking for Banxico to heap
more fuel on the peso’s ﬁre with a rate cut today. Such a move
could see USD/MXN push to 19. Either way, Banxico has slashed the
peso’s rate protection to ultra-low levels, and the peso remains very
vulnerable to global shocks.
Chris Turner
EUR: Knot pitches his hawkish agenda

### Page 3

Klaas Knot delivered a few remarks yesterday after the Netherlands
oﬃcially backed his candidacy for the ECB presidency. He was
ﬁrmly hawkish, claiming second-round inﬂation eﬀects will
probably pick up, and that the ECB will need to respond with higher
rates to small shocks that will lift inﬂation. We’ll wait to hear from
Pablo Hernández de Cos, who is the other frontrunner for the job,
and a long-standing dove. An appointment of Knot would be a

EUR-positive event given his hawkish views, in our view.
Meanwhile, eurozone inﬂation accelerated from 3.8% to 4.0% as
expected in October. That brings the ECB closer to a December hike,
as argued here by our macro team, even if core inﬂation remained
anchored at 2.5%.
As mentioned in the USD section, the EUR:USD two-year swap rate
gap widened against the euro yesterday as oil prices tumbled. A
similar dynamic prevented EUR/USD from rallying in the summer

when oil fell back to $70. Now, at least one more ECB hike – likely in
December – looks like a done deal, providing a stronger ﬂoor for
eurozone rate expectations. Still, this rate component, combined
with lingering French risk, leaves us unexcited about a break higher
in EUR/USD. We continue to see little upside potential beyond
1.130, our year-end target.
Francesco Pesole
GBP: BoE to resume tightening today
We expect the Bank of England to hike rates today by 25bp to 4.0%

(full preview here), in line with market expectations and consensus.
A roughly 25% rise in household energy bills in January now looks
increasingly likely, and we expect headline inﬂation to peak just
above 4% early next year. That has often been indicated as the
pain threshold for the BoE. Still, evidence of second-round
inﬂationary eﬀects remains limited.
We expect a 7-2 vote for a hike, and don’t see the BoE committing
to further tightening at this stage. A follow-up move in February

remains our base case, but policymakers should keep the
emphasis ﬁrmly on incoming evidence of energy pass-through into

wages and prices. An explicit signal that further hikes are likely
would therefore constitute a hawkish surprise.
We think today’s hike can validate the pound’s recent gains, for
now. However, a February hike is fully priced in, and the bar for a
hawkish surprise lifting the pound seems fairly high. We don’t see
immediate rebound potential for EUR/GBP, which is still dampened
by the French eﬀect, but GBP/USD is facing a less supportive short-
term rate diﬀerential, as the GBP curve shows greater dovish

ﬂexibility when oil prices drop. We currently expect stabilisation
around 0.840 in EUR/GBP, while Cable may see its upside capped at
1.350 in the near term.
Francesco Pesole
NOK: Norges Bank on hold today
Norges Bank should keep rates on hold today. Having been one of
the quickest central banks to respond to fresh inﬂationary risks and
given rates are already at an elevated 4.5%, the bar for further
tightening remains fairly high. Incidentally, September CPI ﬁgures

were a bit softer than expected, with underlying inﬂation staying at
3.0% and headline accelerating less than expected to 3.4%.
These are still elevated ﬁgures, so don’t expect Norges Bank to close
the door to rate hikes yet. The threat of further action, if needed, will
remain for some time, but we do not expect any further tightening
in Norway, in line with Norges Bank’s September rate projections.
Markets are pricing in just under 50% chances of another hike by

March.
The krone is facing some headwinds due to the big drop in oil
prices. However, it’s protected by much less hawkish pricing than
other central banks, therefore facing less dovish pressure on energy
price corrections. Yesterday, the NOK two-year swap rate dropped
only 2bp. While this oil move can take EUR/NOK back to 10.50, a
return to $80-85/bll may be needed for a return to the late
September 10.90 levels. We see the pair ending the year in the

10.55-10.65 area.

### Page 5

In Sweden, following yesterday’s 25bp rate hike, inﬂation ﬁgures for
October came in well below consensus. CPIF slowed more than
expected to 1.1%, with the core measure excluding energy
plummeting to 0.1% YoY from 0.5%. These were preliminary
ﬁgures, and expectations are anyway of a pickup to above 2.0% in
both CPIF and the ex-energy measure in 2027, in our view, justifying
another hike in February. That likely explains the contained SEK
reaction to the print. Still, these ﬁgures support our view of a

consolidation around 11.00 in EUR/SEK as opposed to another big
leg lower.
Francesco Pesole
CEE: Lower energy prices overshadow
NBP and CNB meetings
In Poland, the NBP left interest rates unchanged at 3.75%
yesterday, as expected, with the accompanying statement
presenting a mixed picture. On one hand, the forecast included a
signiﬁcant upward revision to the inﬂation outlook; on the other, the
statement noted lower core inﬂation in October and an anticipated

economic slowdown in the third quarter of 2026. Notably, the NBP’s
data cut-oﬀ point was likely two weeks ago. Consequently, the
projections do not account for the downside surprise in October’s
inﬂation ﬁgures, while they do reﬂect record-high energy prices,
which, from today’s perspective, appear somewhat more
favourable. Governor Adam Glapiński’s press conference today is
expected to maintain a dovish tone, and we do not anticipate the
NBP’s ﬁrst rate hike until the ﬁrst quarter of 2027.

In the Czech Republic, we expect the CNB to hike rates by 25bp to
4.00%, the second such increase since the onset of the US-Iran
conﬂict. The CNB remains the most hawkish central bank in the CEE
region, and its new forecast is expected to reinforce this stance. The
updated projections will likely point to higher inﬂation, interest
rates, and wages, alongside a hawkish external environment, while
the GDP growth forecast will likely be revised downward. The CNB

has scaled back its forward guidance. We do not expect ﬁrm
commitments regarding future policy steps today, but rather an

indication that the door remains open for further hikes if necessary.
Given the rally in interest rates over the past few days, the threshold
for a "hawkish" surprise is lower. The market is currently pricing in

roughly 80bp of tightening, down from a peak of 125bp. That said,
the CNB has generally delivered less hawkishness this year than the
market anticipated, often resulting in a weaker currency and lower
interest rates following its meetings.
The global narrative following the US midterms remains robust, and
a sharp drop in oil prices could easily overshadow today’s
meetings of the NBP and the CNB. This sense of relief is supporting
CEE currencies and pushing rates lower. A dovish tone from the NBP

should further fuel the decline in rates, while the zloty will likely
retain room for recovery. The koruna is expected to underperform in
this "risk-on" environment. We see less potential for gains here,
given its lower beta and the fact that the CNB’s stance appears
relatively neutral compared to current market pricing.
Frantisek Taborsky

PROPRIETARY RESEARCH 
EARNINGS AGGREGATES 
STOXX 600 Earnings Outlook provides analysis and commentary on aggregate earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.

Analyst: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA                                         November 5, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com

STOXX 600 EARNINGS OUTLOOK  
EUROPEAN AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS

 Third quarter earnings are expected to increase 25.0% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, earnings 
are expected to increase 9.5%.

 Third quarter revenue is expected to increase 11.9% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, revenues are 
expected to increase 6.9%.

 212 companies in the STOXX 600 have reported earnings to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 58.0% reported results 
exceeding analyst estimates.

 257 companies in the STOXX 600 have reported revenue to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 64.2% reported revenue 
exceeding analyst estimates.

 During the week of November 9, 40 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

Through November 5, 212 companies in the STOXX 600 Index reported earnings for Q3 2026. Of these, 58% reported 
earnings above analyst estimates. In a typical quarter (sin ce 2012), 54% of companies beat estimates and 39% miss 
estimates.

In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 4.2% above estimates, which is below the 5.9% long-term 
(since 2012) average surprise factor.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the STOXX 600 fo r Q3 2026 is 25%. The STOXX 600 expects to see share-
weighted earnings of €164.4B in Q3 2026, compared to shar e-weighted earnings of €131.6B (based on the year-ago 
earnings of the current constituents) in Q3 2025.

Eight of the ten sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to Q3 2025. The Energy sector 
has the highest earnings growth rate for the quarter, wh ile the Real Estate has the weakest anticipated growth 
compared to Q3 2025.

The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (153 .4%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €35.7B in Q3 
2026, compared to earnings of €14.1B in Q3 2025.

The Real Estate sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (-69.6%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €0.9B in Q3 
2026, compared to earnings of €3.1B in Q3 2025.

sales of €196.8B in Q3 2026, compared to sales of €199.1B in Q3 2025.

ANALYST: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA October 30, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com; 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 31.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.4%. 
 Of the 299 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 81.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 13.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 12.2%. 
 69.9% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 66 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 66 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 19.0. 
 During the week of Nov. 2, 144 S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings. 
SECTION A: EARNINGS OUTLOOK

This Week in Earnings provides analysis and commentary on aggregate earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.  2 
There has been an increase in the share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 since the start of the quarter (to $778.4B 
from $760.7B). Three of the eleven sectors have experienced downward revisions to estimates.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-20.5%) and materials (-7%) sectors have recorded the highest percentage 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (33%) sector has recorded the highest percentage increase in earnings. 
Overall, share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 2.3% since the start of the quarter.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-14.7B) and materials (-1.2B) sectors have recorded the highest dollar-level 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (15.7B) sector has recorded the highest dollar-level increase in earnings. 
Overall, expected share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 17.7B since the start of the quarter.

299 companies in the S&P 500 Index have reported earnings for Q3 2026. Of these companies, 81.6% reported 
earnings above analyst expectations and 15.7% reported earnings below analyst expectations. In a typical quarter 
(since 1994), 67% of companies beat estimates and 20% miss estimates. Over the past four quarters, 82% of 
companies beat the estimates and 15% missed estimates. 
In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 4.5% above estimates, which compares to a long-term (since 
1994) average surprise factor of 4.4% and the average surprise factor over the prior four quarters of 7.7%.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 31.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 26.4%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $778.4B in 26Q3, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $591.4B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 constituents) in 25Q3. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q3. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Consumer 
Discretionary sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (146.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $63B in 26Q3, 
compared to earnings of $25.6B in 25Q3. 4 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (566.5%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (143.8%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 39%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (68.9%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $260.2B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $154B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (137%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (71.5%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 28.2%. 
The Consumer Discretionary sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.9%) of any sector. It is expected to earn 
$56.3B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $54.2B in 25Q3. 7 of the 18 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to 
see lower earnings than a year ago. The Casinos & Gaming (-44.1%) and Automotive Parts & Equipment (-38.8%) 
sub-industries have the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate 
improves to 4.5%.

than a year ago. The Aerospace & Defense (-39.6%) and Passenger Airlines (-29.8%) sub-industries have the lowest 
earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate improves to 30.5%.

STARMINE EARNINGS SURPRISE FORECAST
Looking forward at quarterly performance, we use StarMine’s SmartEstimate® from LSEG to determine which
companies in the S&P 500 are better poised to beat and miss earnings estimates. The SmartEstimate® is a weighted

average of analyst estimates, with more weight given to more recent estimates and more accurate analysts. Our
studies have shown that when the SmartEstimate® differs significantly from the consensus (I/B/E/S mean estimate),
the Predicted Surprise accurately predicts the direction of earnings surprises or further revisions 70% of the time.
When a significant Predicted Surprise for revenue is also present for the period, the accuracy improves to 78%.

StarMine ARM from LSEG is an analyst revisions stock ranking model, designed to predict future changes in analyst
sentiment. ARM incorporates more accurate earnings estimates through the SmartEstimate prediction service. ARM
region rankings scores companies by region on a scale of 1 to 100 where 100 represents the most bullish sentiment.
Over the next two weeks, 147 S&P 500 companies are expected to report earnings. Of these companies, 17 positive

surprises and 11 negative surprises are expected from S&P 500 companies reporting quarterly results.

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Charts of the week: No capitulation

A hotter-than-expected US inflation report triggered a pullback in risk assets, but there are few signs of capitulation so far 
US core and supercore inflation; US inflation breadth*    Our ‘Fear and Greed’ indicator

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *share of US consumer inflation components rising more than 3%

Editorial 
Staying defensive and diversified  
Strategy summary:  Two weeks ago, we turned defensive 
towards equities, locking in semiconductor equity gains amid 
elevated bond volatility, narrowing market breadth and political 
uncertainty before the US mid -term elections. The near -term 
outlook reflected a less favourable risk-reward balance. Since 
we turned defensive , the Nasdaq 100 index has recorded its 
biggest weekly decline in three months as September’s hotter-
than-expected US inflation report briefly lifted bond yields. 
We have extended our defensive stance this week, 
downgrading US Communication Services equities to Neutral 
and upgrading US Healthcare to Overweight. We also closed 
our opportunistic bullish Communication Services idea and 
initiated a bullish idea on Healthcare, where earnings visibility 
and defensive characteristics appear more attractive. In fixed 
income, we see opportunities in US 10-year government bonds. 
We await clearer AI profit margins and spending guidance, 
broader earnings growth or lower bond and equity market 
volatility before turning positive on equities. Until then, 
diversification and disciplined entry levels remain important. 
Hot US inflation triggers equity pullback; not hot enough 
for an October Fed hike:  Risk assets pulled back after 
September’s hotter-than-expected inflation data (headline 3.7% 
y/y; core 2.6% y/y). Higher gasoline and diesel prices were the 
main drivers, but passthrough appeared in airline fares, used 
cars and transportation services, showing pressures were not 
confined to energy. Almost half of inflation components are still 
rising above 3%, a measure recently cited by Fed Chair Warsh. 
Producer inflation also beat estimates. The reports revive the 
possibility of an October Fed rate hike, but we expect a divided 
Fed to weigh it against September’s soft jobs report and 
narrowly hold this month. A December hike appears more likely 
if inflation remains firm . Although Fed Chair Warsh did not 
comment on policy at an IMF speech this week, New York Fed 
chief Williams said he saw no need to hike in 2027. Our base 
case remains two further hikes by June 2027. 
Limited equity pullback so far.  Equities fell sharply after the 
US inflation report, but the pullback is limited. The Nasdaq 100 
is 4.5% below its record high, while the S&P 500 remains close 
to all -time highs despite the latest decline. The S&P 500 
volatility index (VIX) has risen from below 15 to 1 8.2, still well 
under the 25 –30 range typically associated with capitulation 
and forced selling. Meanwhile, US government bond volatility 
(MOVE) remains elevated after surging since September.  Our 
stock-bond quant model turned more negativ e (-2) this week, 
signalling elevated equity volatility in the near term.  
When to turn more constructive on stocks?  We would turn 
more constructive when capitulation signals align: the Nasdaq 
100 approaches a 10% drawdown, the VIX rises to 25 –30 and 
our ‘Fear and Greed’ index  enters “extreme fear”. Greater 
confidence would also require easing concerns about AI 
spending and technology firms’ exposure to neocloud revenue, 
lower bond volatility and our stock-bond model turning positive 
again. Until these conditions emerge, volatility may remain 
elevated, favouring a defensive equity stance. Political risk 
should ease after the US mid -term elections. We continue to 
seek selective opportunities following excessive corrections. 
US financials remain a preferred sector after Q3 earnings:  
US large banks’ earnings were mixed, but underlying trends 
remain encouraging. Healthy loan and deposit growth supports 
net interest income, while resilient consumer spending, wealth 
management activity and a solid M&A and refinancing pipeline 
strengthen the outlook. Credit risks appear manageable . We 
remain constructive and favour stronger, diversified franchises. 
US government bonds offer value as yields spike:  The 
reversal in US government bond yields after their initial inflation-
driven spike suggests limited further upside. The 10 -year yield 
briefly reached 5.32% before slumping as falling equities 
prompted short-covering. We expect a near -term 5.00–5.25% 
range and view moves above 5.25% as attractive entry points 
for our opportunistic 10-year government bond idea. Structural 
pressures, including term premium and increasing supply to 
fund fiscal deficits and AI investments, prevent us from turning 
outright bullish on longer maturities. We, therefore, continue to 
prefer 3–7-year maturities in broader bond allocations.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori

Top client questions 
  How does the September 2026 US Consumer Price Index 
(CPI) report alter your view on the USD and bond yields?   
Our view: The hotter-than-expected CPI print raises the odds of 
an October Fed hike, though we still expect a hold followed by 
a rate hike in December. In the near term, we maintain our view 
that the US 10 -year government bond yield will range between 
5.00% and 5.25%, while  the USD will remain supported as 
markets reassess the likelihood of further Fed hikes. Additional 
gains would depend partly on markets pricing a faster or more 
prolonged tightening cycle, while clearer signs of an 
approaching policy rate peak could see the USD ease back. 
Rationale: September US consumer inflation  came in above 
consensus across the board – headline CPI rose 3.7% y/y (vs. 3.6% 
estimate), and core CPI rose 2.6% y/y (vs. 2.5% estimate) – marking 
the fastest headline acceleration in five months. This shifts the 
balance of risks modestly hawkish . Unsurprisingly, the market -
implied probability of an October Fed hike rose over 50% overnight 
but closed the day at 32.7% – 9ppt higher than Tuesday’s close. 
We believe a split Fed will weigh this inflation report against the soft 
September jobs report and narrowly vote to keep rates on hold this 
month. Our base case remains for two more Fed hikes  by June 
2027. A December rate hike  now looks more like a done deal, 
especially as persistently higher oil prices keep headline and core 
inflation elevated for at least the next few months. Any re-escalation 
of the Middle East conflict after the  US mid-term elections would 
exert pressure on the Fed, although we believe Republicans would  
show greater restraint in US foreign action if they suffer steep Senate 
losses, which looks increasingly likely. 
The intra-day price action in US government bonds was instructive. 
The US 10-year yield briefly spiked to 5.32% on the print before 
closing materially lower, as pre -existing bearish positioning was 
unwound and Fed speakers (Barkin, Goolsbee) stopped well short 
of signalling an imminent move. This dynamic reinforces our view 
that the technical selling pressure driving yields towards the upper 
end of our range is fading. Structural drivers – term premium, fiscal 
supply concerns and ‘higher for longer’ Fed repricing – remain intact 
and keep us from turning outright bullish on duration. However, our 
opportunistic bullish idea in US 10-year government bonds initiated 
at September-end remains, as we continue to view spikes in US 10-
year yields over 5.25% as a good risk-reward entry. From a longer-
term portfolio standpoint, we continue to favour the 3-7-year tenor. 
On FX, elevated US government bond yields should sustain the 
USD’s interest rate appeal. However, expected hikes alone will not 
drive further appreciation. Stronger data or firmer Fed guidance 
would provide fresh support, while evidence that tightening is 
nearing completion would temper the USD’s strength. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are your key takeaways from US bank earnings? 
Our view: Q3 2026 earnings results from major US banks have 
been mixed, but underlying trends remain encouraging. Healthy 
loan and deposit growth, resilient consumer spending and 
wealth management activity continue to support the sector. 
Credit risks remain manageable. We remain constructive on the 
US financial sector, favouring stronger, diversified franchises. 
Rationale: Healthy loan and deposit growth continue to support net 
interest income (NII), hinting that the US economy remains resilient. 
Capital markets revenue grew year -on-year (y/y) , but moderated 
sequentially after a very strong Q2 2026, although performance 
varied across banks. Management commentary points to a healthy 
deal pipeline , supported by M&A activity and debt refinancing 
demand, although execution will depend on market conditions. 
Provisioning and net charge -off trends were mixed across banks, 
with no clear signs of broad deterioration in credit quality. Firmer 
US inflation raises the risk of further monetary tightening. While 
higher rates can support asset yields, they can also increase 
funding costs, pressure borrowers and weigh on securities ’ 
valuations. We will continue to monitor lending demand, credit 
costs, NII trends, capital markets momentum and capital returns.  
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Can Big Tech’s earnings guidance sustain confidence in 
the AI investment cycle? 
Our view: We expect Big Tech’s AI spending to remain elevated, 
supporting our constructive AI capex outlook. However, we 
await guidance on the pace of capex growth and the 
sustainability of profit margins. 
Rationale: Semiconductor leaders’ latest company guidance points 
to supply constraints extending through 2028, supporting continued 
capacity expansion for compute . However, while AI capex is still 
likely to rise, y/y growth is expected to moderate  from c.100% in 
Q3 2026 to c.20% in Q3 2027. This normalisation in growth could 
raise concerns about the AI investment cycle’s durability.  
The Nasdaq-100 Index has rallied alongside accelerating AI capex. 
Slower capex growth is likely to moderate semiconductor revenue 
growth, while hyperscalers continue to incur depreciation costs on 
past investments. The slower growth is already partially reflected in 
the Nasdaq -100’s valuation de -rating this year. While we remain 
constructive on the AI investment theme, we prefer to stay 
prudent in the near term and await Big Tech’s guidance for greater 
visibility into spending plans in H2 2027 and beyond. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would France face a credit rating downgrade by rating 
agencies this month? What might be the market implications? 
Our view: A rating downgrade is unlikely unless France’s 2027 
budget passes the National Assembly very soon with material 
changes to the proposal. We see French government bond 
yields remaining elevated relative to German Bund yields. 
Rationale: The French National Assembly began debating France’s 
2027 draft budget on 13 October 2026, with the first vote scheduled 
for 20 October. Near-term budget approval appears unlikely, as 
opposition parties have tabled motions to reject the proposal. The 
French Parliament has up to 70 days from the budget’s submission 
to complete the legislative process, although  its final adoption and 
the resolution of political challenges could extend into early 2027.  
Last October, two of the three major rating agencies downgraded 
France by one notch to the high-single-A category  with stable 
outlooks. The remaining agenc y rated France in the low-AA 
category with a negative outlook. As negative outlooks are typically 
resolved within 12 -18 months, agenc ies have time to await fiscal 
clarity before acting. We believe downgrade risks are largely priced 
in after the recent rise in yields, but volatility is likely to persist amid 
fiscal uncertainty and the approaching presidential election. Banks 
and domestically-focused issuers would face the greatest near-term 
pressure. However, we would view wider spreads as an opportunity 
to add exposure, particularly to French banks’ subordinated 
debt, given their stronger balance sheets and the fact that France 
contributes less than 50% of major French banks’ overall revenue. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Japanese banks? 
Our view: We are positive on Japanese banks despite the recent 
market correction, as the structural drivers of improving 
profitability and shareholder returns remain intact . While 
political risk and shifting BoJ rate hike odds could sustain near-
term volatility, we see the recent pullback as an opportunity to 
build selective exposure to Japanese bank stocks. 
Rationale: Japan’s gradual monetary policy normalisation  
should support banks’ NII as floating-rate loans reprice and maturing 
assets are reinvested at higher yields. Healthy corporate lending 
demand, improving capital efficiency and continued share buybacks 
provide further support . While uncertainty over the pace of BoJ 
tightening could weigh on sentiment, we see gradual interest rate 
normalisation as supportive of medium-term bank profitability.  
Key risks  include rising deposit costs, mark -to-market losses on 
bond portfolios and a disorderly increase in Japanese government 
bond yields. We continue to favour well-capitalised banks  with 
strong domestic deposit franchises and improving returns on equity. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Could NZD/USD rebound from its one-year trough? If so, 
how far could it recover?  
Our view: New Zealand’s improving domestic conditions, 
persistent inflation and technical support provide a basis for 
NZD/USD’s initial recovery towards the 0.57 level, although 
elevated US bond yields could slow its progress. 
Rationale: In Q3, New Zealand’s  seasonally-adjusted business 
confidence reading rose to a net 40% of firms expecting the economy 
to improve, up from net 14% in Q2 . Hiring intentions improved, and 
filled jobs rose 0.2% m/m in August. These developments suggest 
domestic conditions remain supportive for the NZD.  
New Zealand’s above-target inflation keeps further RBNZ tightening 
in focus. The 22 October consumer inflation data release and the 
28 October RBNZ policy meeting are key catalysts: persistent price 
pressures a nd improving demand are expected to prompt firmer 
policy guidance and strengthen the NZD’s interest-rate appeal. 
NZD/USD’s position near the lower boundary of its downtrend 
channel suggests scope for a technical rebound. We see 0.57 as 
the first recovery hurdle; a sustained break  above this level  would 
strengthen the case for further gains. Firmer US  consumer inflation 
remains a headwind, making a gradual recovery more plausible 
than a sharp reversal.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  Can the proposed Russia -Ukraine ‘energy ceasefire ’ 
contain oil prices as Middle East risks rise? 
Our view: We expect escalating Middle East tensions to keep oil 
prices elevated . We  maintain our  three- and 12 -month West 
Texas Intermediate (WTI) oil price forecasts at USD 100/bbl and 
USD 80/bbl, respectively, and continue to favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a portfolio hedge 
against persistent inflation risks. 
Rationale: The proposed Russia-Ukraine ‘energy ceasefire’ still 
looks some way off. In our view, even if an agreement is reached, it 
would do little to ease the supply risks building in the Middle East. 
Gulf oil exports have recovered to above 80% of pre-conflict levels 
in recent weeks, helping moderate the pace of price increases. 
However, the recovery remains vulnerable to disruptions, particularly 
following the latest Houthi attacks on Saudi energy infrastructure . 
Diesel and jet fuel remain under the most pressure , with Gulf 
exports of these fuels in September  at only around 60% of pre -
conflict levels. A sustained disruption to major Saudi production or 
export infrastructure remains the principal upside risk to oil prices. 
—  Anthony Naab, Investment Strategist

Document 456: Why China resists rebalancing its growth model_ (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Why China resists rebalancing its growth model_ (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.39 / 26 October 2026EDITORIAL
WHY CHINA RESISTS REBALANCING ITS GROWTH MODEL?
China has never exported as much as it does now, and its trade surplus has never been higher. At the same time, its
economic growth is slowing, held back by weak domestic demand. The imbalances in China’s growth model, and the ex-
ternal imbalances they give rise to, are therefore continuing to worsen. With Europe taking a tougher line and the United

States, with which China shares nothing more than ‘strategic stability’, the standoff looks set to continue. However, the
European Union and China would have a great deal to lose from a lasting deterioration in their relationship. It is in the
interests of both parties to seek to restore the balance. To this end, it is important to understand Beijing’s objectives and
the constraints facing China. Why does the country resist adjusting its growth model? Whilst exports are a vital asset for

sustaining economic activity and asserting China’s technological and industrial power, this strategy also comes at a cost.
IMBALANCES ARE WORSENING
A K-shaped economy. China’s economic growth slowed to 4.6% year-
on-year (y/y) over the first three quarters of 2026, from 5.0% in 2025.
More worryingly, the gap is widening between weak domestic demand
and the very strong performance of exports, driven by artificial intelli-
gence equipment and green technologies. Furthermore, the dynamism

of export-oriented industries has not, so far, had any positive knock-on
effects on other sectors or on households.
Domestic demand is struggling. The property crisis, now in its sixth
year, and a sluggish labour market are weighing on household confi -
dence and consumption. Since the start of 2026, households have been
prioritising debt reduction (bank loans to households have fallen by
1% y/y), house sales have continued to fall, and retail sales have been

stagnating (Chart 1). On the investment front, the decline is becoming
more pronounced and widespread: it is affecting not only the property
sector but also infrastructure and many manufacturing sectors (with
the exception of the textile industry, transport and communications,
and electronic and electrical equipment).
Exports remain very buoyant. Exports continue to be the main driver of
growth (their net contribution is expected to account for around a third

of real GDP growth in 2026, as it did in 2024–2025). Against a backdrop
of rebound in global trade, Chinese companies maintained their market
share at 14.6% of total exports (measured in US dollars) in the first
half of 2026, as they had in 2025. Growth in goods exports accelerated
(21.9% y/y in USD over the first nine months, compared with 5.2% in
2025), driven by both volumes and prices. These have rebounded after
three years of decline, driven by rising costs of raw materials and the

prices of goods required for AI, but they have increased at a slower rate
than the global price average. Exports from high-tech sectors (batte -
ries, electric vehicles, robots and AI equipment) are posting the highest
growth rates (+55.8% y/y for all AI-related goods over the first nine
months of 2026).
Record external surpluses. Imports, which had been stagnating since
2022, have been recovering since the start of the year (up 27.2% y/y
over the first nine months), also driven by both volumes and prices.

The countries benefiting from this rebound in the Chinese market are
commodity exporters and Asian producers integrated into AI value
chains (South Korea in particular). Otherwise, China has significantly
reduced its reliance on imports over the past few years. Overall, the
trade surplus has grown at a slower rate in 2026 than in previous years.
Nevertheless, it has still reached a record high: USD1,249 billion over
the twelve months to September 2026, which is three times its 2019

level (see Chart 2).
SOURCE: CPB, NBS, BNP PARIBAS

### Page 2

4
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.39 / 26 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Meanwhile, the current account surplus is expected to approach a re -
cord level of USD 800 billion in 2026, representing nearly 4% of China’s
GDP (a ratio already seen in the late 2000s). As a proportion of global
GDP , this is unprecedented (at 0.6%).
Imbalances set to persist. The imbalances inherent in the growth mo-
del are likely to persist, fuelled notably by the strong price and non-

price competitiveness of Chinese goods. This competitiveness remains
underpinned by: i/ innovation efforts and the move up the value chain
in high-tech sectors and the low-carbon transition; ii/ low production
costs (labour, low-cost credit, land and energy, tax benefits 1); iii/ the
yuan’s exchange rate. Its slow appreciation since mid-2025 has not
offset the cumulative depreciation from 2022 to 2025 (nominal and
real depreciation due to low inflation), and its real effective exchange

rate (REER) remains 10% below its 2021 average. At the end of 2025,
the IMF estimated that the REER was undervalued by between 12%
and 20%.
Beijing’s economic strategy is exacerbating imbalances. The 15th Five-
Year Plan2, launched this year, prioritises innovation, high-tech (ex -
port-oriented) sectors and technological self-reliance above all else,
whilst reforms to the social security system and private consumption
remain on the back burner (see Chart 3). Furthermore, fiscal policy,

which had provided moderate support for household spending for a
time in 2025, was tightened in 2026 (until the recent announcements
of new measures for the final quarter).
WHY BEIJING IS DIGGING ITS HEELS IN
Beijing is not giving ground on any key issues. Faced with trading
partners calling on China to rebalance its economic model, reduce in -
dustrial subsidies and strengthen the yuan, Beijing responds that the
trade surplus reflects the competitiveness of its goods, points to the

United States’ insufficient savings, and uses critical materials as a bar-
gaining chip in negotiations.
The need for growth. Exports are a key driver of growth at a time when
the Chinese economy is undergoing a period of transition. The country
has moved away from its pre-2022 model, which was based on in -
vestment in property and traditional industries and financed by debt.
Growth is now driven by ‘new productive forces’, and exports must pro-

vide producers with the market that domestic demand does not. Fur -
thermore, innovation and the roll-out of AI must stimulate productivity
gains and offset the impact of the shrinking labour force on economic
growth. It is essential to limit the slowdown of growth in order to sup-
port job creation and absorb the economy’s excess debt.
The strategic argument. Exports are a means of asserting China’s in -
dustrial and technological supremacy, weakening its competitors, and

strengthening ‘national security’. They place China at the heart of va -
lue chains in the most strategic industries, such as AI and the low-car-
bon transition. Furthermore, the pursuit of self-sufficiency and import
substitution — which exacerbate external imbalances — also help to
build national sovereignty by reducing dependencies.
A political and fiscal choice. Expanding the social safety net is seen as
a solution for providing long-term support for household consumption3

by reducing their savings rate (estimated at 35.7% of disposable income
in 2025, which is more than double the average rate for the euro area).
1 According to the IMF, the cost of China’s industrial policy amounts to about 4% of GDP . See IMF, ‘Industrial Policy in China: Quantification and Impact on Misalloca-
tion’, August 2025: https://doi.org/10.5089/9798229017855.001
2 See BNP Paribas, EcoWeek, China’s 15th Five-Year Plan: prioritising power over addressing imbalances

3 The IMF estimates, for example, that doubling social expenditure (health, education, social services and basic pensions) in rural areas could reduce savings and
raise the ratio of private consumption to GDP by 1.8 pp in the medium term, at an annual fiscal cost of 1% of GDP . See IMF, People’s Republic of China 2025 Article IV,
February 2026: https://doi.org/10.5089/9798229038911.002
However, this is not a priority for Beijing, certainly due to ‘ideologi -

cal’ mistrust of the welfare state and a desire for fiscal prudence.
A major reform of the healthcare or pension systems would place a
strain on public finances in both the short and long term, particularly
as the population ages. Whilst the central government remains in a
sound financial position, local governments are already under strain
following several years of falling land sales proceeds, high deficits and
rising debt. The authorities do not have a large fiscal room for ma -

noeuvre and wish to preserve it in anticipation of future shocks.
LIMITATIONS AND POSSIBLE ADJUSTMENTS
In China, a society gripped by anxiety… Economic imbalances are ta -
king their toll on households. The emphasis placed on manufacturing
rather than services is having a negative impact on employment and
wages (the share of value added allocated to employees’ pay is esti -
mated at 50% in the services sector and 41% in manufacturing). The
downturn in the property market is eroding households’ wealth, and

their savings are mainly held in low-yield bank accounts. Finally, social
spending is rising slowly (by 5.2% a year since 2020, compared with
9.7% over the previous five years).
… and enterprises under pressure. The low prices of Chinese goods,
both for export and on the domestic market — resulting from excess
production capacity and ‘involution’ — are eroding corporate profit
margins. Total profits for industrial enterprises have recovered this
year but they are highly concentrated, particularly in AI-related sec -

tors, while the proportion of industrial companies reporting losses has
continued to rise (30%, compared with 26% in 2022). These vulnerabili-
ties are having increasing contagion effects on the rest of the economy,
notably through worsening payment delays and rising pressure on the
profitability of financial institutions.
